In this paper, we propose \textbf{PailitaoGR}, a \emph{Latent Think-with-Images} method for generative image retrieval, which internalizes target-focused perception and selective auxiliary-evidence utilization into a the generative retrieval model, enabling \textit{Zooming without Cropping} and \textit{Reading without OCR}. Specifically, we design a target-focused perception mechanism that identifies and enhances visual tokens of the search target, consisting of a target Enhancer and a learning strategy based on on-policy distillation and attention guidance loss, enabling the model to focus on search-target regions. We also design a selective auxiliary-evidence utilization mechanism that identifies and enhances visual tokens of auxiliary evidence, including an auxiliary enhancer and an in-capacity incremental contrastive distillation strategy, enabling the model to exploit auxiliary evidence. We construct training and validation sets sampled from real-world online image-search logs. Experiments show that our method outperforms existing baselines by an average of 13.8\%, validating its effectiveness.

</details>


### [27] [Hull First, Wake Second: Wake-Reliance Suppression for Robust Maritime Vessel Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26665)
*Yefan Wang,Xingyu Wang,Ruibiao Zhu,Yusen Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HullWake is a hull-first wake-second framework for robust maritime vessel detection that addresses the wake-reliance problem where detectors miss weak-wake vessels or produce false positives on wake-like water clutter. It separates hull and wake evidence extraction, uses bidirectional proposal-anchored corridors for wake cues, and suppresses wake-dominant predictions through multiple mechanisms. The method is evaluated on Curated-Wake (10,000 images) with new wake annotations, showing improvements across multiple metrics including overall AP, weak/no-wake robustness, and confidence stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Maritime vessel detectors often face scenes where hulls are small, low-contrast, or blurred while wakes are longer and easier to detect. This creates a "wake-reliance problem": detectors may miss slow or stationary vessels with weak wakes, or produce false positives on wake-like water clutter. Existing detectors rely too heavily on wake features rather than learning robust hull representations.

Method: HullWake proposes a hull-first wake-second framework with four key components: (1) Separates proposal-centered hull evidence from directional wake context; (2) Extracts wake cues using bidirectional proposal-anchored corridors; (3) Suppresses wake-dominant predictions through wake response supervision, wake-attenuated consistency, wake-only confidence suppression, and hull-wake decorrelation. The evaluation protocol covers weak/no-wake vessels, wake-like hard negatives, worst-group AP, and confidence drop after wake attenuation.

Result: HullWake improves overall AP, weak/no-wake robustness, wake-like false positives, worst-group AP, and confidence stability after wake attenuation compared to box-only detectors and mask-supervised segmentation baselines. The method is evaluated on Curated-Wake, a wake-oriented maritime dataset of about 10,000 images curated from Ships/Vessels in Aerial Images, SMD benchmark, and SeaDronesSee, with newly added detection- and segmentation-level wake annotations.

Conclusion: The hull-first wake-second approach effectively addresses the wake-reliance problem in maritime vessel detection. By explicitly decoupling hull and wake features and suppressing wake-dominant predictions, HullWake achieves more robust detection across diverse vessel scenarios, especially for weak/no-wake vessels and challenging wake-like clutter conditions.

Abstract: Maritime vessel detectors often face scenes where hulls are small, low-contrast, or blurred, while wakes are longer and easier to detect. This creates a wake-reliance problem: detectors may miss slow or stationary vessels with weak wakes, or produce false positives on wake-like water clutter. We propose HullWake, a hull-first wake-second framework for robust maritime vessel detection. HullWake separates proposal-centered hull evidence from directional wake context, extracts wake cues with bidirectional proposal-anchored corridors, and suppresses wake-dominant predictions through wake response supervision, wake-attenuated consistency, wake-only confidence suppression, and hull--wake decorrelation. We also introduce a wake-oriented evaluation protocol covering weak/no-wake vessels, wake-like hard negatives, worst-group AP, and confidence drop after wake attenuation. Experiments are conducted on Curated-Wake, a wake-oriented maritime dataset of about 10,000 images curated from Ships/Vessels in Aerial Images, the SMD benchmark, and SeaDronesSee, with newly added detection- and segmentation-level wake annotations. Compared with box-only detectors and mask-supervised segmentation baselines, HullWake improves overall AP, weak/no-wake robustness, wake-like false positives, worst-group AP, and confidence stability after wake attenuation.

</details>


### [28] [RECAP-Forcing: Retaining Content Appearances for Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26671)
*Haiyang Xu,Zheng Ding,Zhuowen Tu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RECAP-Forcing is a training-free inference method for long autoregressive video generation that organizes memory by appearance novelty rather than temporal recency. It retains KV cache for newly appearing content (entering subjects, disoccluded regions, new scenes) at first visibility, scaling memory with novel content rather than video length.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long autoregressive video generation faces a fundamental memory challenge: with finite attention window, models must decide which information to retain from expanding history. Existing methods organize memory temporally (preserving recent frames), but this loses older important information. The key insight is that a long video is an evolving cast of subjects, objects, and scenes whose identities must remain consistent over time.

Method: The method organizes memory by appearance novelty. It uses two mechanisms: (1) At video start, an attention sink preserves the initial scene when all content is novel; (2) As video evolves, an optical-flow-based novelty bank selectively retains newly revealed content. No additional learnable parameters; training-free inference.

Result: Consistently improves visual quality and semantic fidelity across multiple strong baselines and outperforms existing memory methods.

Conclusion: Appearance-indexed memory makes long-range consistency an explicit property of the memory structure. Memory scales with newly introduced content rather than video length, providing a unified framework that addresses the memory challenge in long video generation without requiring training.

Abstract: Long autoregressive video generation faces a fundamental memory challenge: with a finite attention window, a model must decide which information from an ever-expanding history to retain. Existing methods organize memory temporally, preserving recent frames while compressing or discarding older ones. We instead propose RECAP-Forcing, organizing memory by appearance novelty. A long video is not merely a sequence of frames, but an evolving cast of subjects, objects, and scenes whose identities must remain consistent over time. We organize memory by retaining the KV cache associated with newly appearing content--such as entering subjects, disoccluded regions, and newly introduced scenes--at the moment it first becomes visible, prioritizing novelty over recency. Memory should scale with the amount of newly introduced content, rather than with video length. This appearance-indexed memory makes long-range consistency an explicit property of the memory structure. Our framework unifies two mechanisms under this single principle. At the beginning of a video, when all visible content is novel, an attention sink preserves the initial scene. As the video evolves, an optical-flow-based novelty bank extends the same principle by selectively retaining newly revealed content. As a training-free inference method with no additional learnable parameters, RECAP-Forcing consistently improves visual quality and semantic fidelity across multiple strong baselines and outperforms existing memory methods.

</details>


### [29] [Reason in the Words You Speak: Idiolectal Paraphrasing Off-Policy Traces for Reasoning Distillation in VideoLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26684)
*Ji Soo Lee,Jinyoung Park,Seohyun Lee,Jongha Kim,Joonmyung Choi,Jinsung Yoon,Hyunwoo J. Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Echo-GRPO addresses the distribution mismatch problem in reasoning distillation by rewriting teacher model reasoning traces into the student model's own linguistic style (idiolect) while preserving semantics, improving reasoning learning without gradient clipping issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: GRPO is on-policy and bounded by existing reasoning capabilities; using off-policy privileged traces from teacher models causes distribution mismatch that leads to gradient clipping on critical reasoning tokens, preventing learning of the reasoning process even when answers are correct.

Method: Echo-GRPO uses Dual-Reference Decoding to rewrite teacher reasoning traces into the student policy's characteristic vocabulary and expression patterns (idiolect) while preserving semantics, instantiated as VideoEcho-R1 for video reasoning distillation.

Result: Consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks. The idiolectal paraphrasing module also improves both RL and supervised fine-tuning frameworks for reasoning distillation.

Conclusion: Echo-GRPO successfully bridges the on-policy/off-policy gap in reasoning distillation by aligning teacher traces with student policy distribution, enabling effective learning of reasoning processes. This approach extends beyond GRPO to benefit other reasoning distillation frameworks.

Abstract: Recent large language models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, where reinforcement learning with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has emerged as a leading paradigm for optimizing models on self-generated trajectories. However, the on-policy nature of GRPO bounds the model to the reasoning skills it can already produce, restricting to learn more advanced capabilities. Prior works inject privileged reasoning traces from a stronger teacher policy to guide training, yet these traces are inherently out of distribution with respect to the student policy. We observe that this mismatch between on-policy and off-policy causes gradient clipping on semantically critical reasoning tokens, ultimately rewarding correct answers while leaving the reasoning that justifies them unlearned. Hence, we propose \textbf{Echo-GRPO}, a framework that lets the model reason in the words it speaks. Rather than imitating low-probability privileged traces from the teacher model, Echo-GRPO rewrites them into the student policy's own \textit{idiolect}, that is, its own characteristic vocabulary and expression patterns, while preserving their semantics via Dual-Reference Decoding. We instantiate this framework as \textbf{VideoEcho-R1} for video reasoning distillation, achieving consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks. Finally, we show that our idiolectal paraphrasing is a plug-in module that consistently improves both RL and supervised fine-tuning frameworks for reasoning distillation, demonstrating that policy-aligned supervision extends beyond GRPO.

</details>


### [30] [Domain-Specific Self-Supervised Representation Learning for Retinal Fundus Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26686)
*Bekzat Nurlanbekova,Fung Fung Ting*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates contrastive self-supervised learning (SimSiam and SimCLR) for retinal disease classification from fundus images under resource-constrained settings, demonstrating that tailored augmentation strategies and lightweight SSL frameworks can learn transferable representations reducing dependence on large annotated datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Annotated medical images are scarce and costly to obtain, while supervised learning methods require large labeled datasets. Contrastive SSL has emerged as a promising alternative to learn useful representations from unlabeled data to address this limitation.

Method: The authors investigate two SSL frameworks (SimSiam and SimCLR) for retinal disease classification from fundus images, focusing on augmentation strategies and training parameters under resource-constrained settings. They explore training with small batch sizes combined with retinal-specific augmentation techniques, evaluating representation quality via linear evaluation and fine-tuning across downstream tasks including multi-disease classification and diabetic retinopathy grading.

Result: Tailoring augmentation strategies to retinal image characteristics plays a critical role in improving performance. Even under constrained settings, lightweight SSL frameworks can learn transferable representations that reduce dependence on large annotated datasets and achieve competitive results.

Conclusion: Self-supervised learning with properly designed augmentations offers a viable path to reduce annotation burden in medical imaging, enabling competitive performance with limited labeled data and computational resources.

Abstract: Despite the growing number of public datasets, annotated medical images remain scarce. Supervised learning methods achieve strong performance on many benchmarks, however require large amounts of labeled data, which are costly and time-consuming to obtain in the medical domain. To address this limitation, contrastive self-supervised learning (SSL) has emerged as a promising alternative for learning useful representations from unlabeled data. In this work, we investigate two SSL frameworks, SimSiam and SimCLR, for retinal disease classification from fundus images. We focus on understanding how augmentation strategies and training parameters influence representation learning under resource-constrained settings. Given limited data and computational capacity, we explore the feasibility of training SSL models with small batch sizes incorporated with retinal-specific augmentation techniques. Through a series of experiments, we assess the quality of learned representations via linear evaluation and fine-tuning across downstream tasks, including multi-disease classification and diabetic retinopathy grading. Our results show that tailoring augmentation strategies to the characteristics of retinal images plays a critical role in improving performance. Even under constrained settings, lightweight SSL frameworks can learn transferable representations that reduce dependence on large annotated datasets and achieve competitive results.

</details>


### [31] [AesCanvas: A Large-Scale Dataset and Benchmark for Aesthetic Critique and Contextual Suitability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26713)
*Xuanwei Hu,Haoyu Dong,Kejun Wu,Tianyi Liu,Jianjun Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AesCanvas introduces a benchmark suite for evaluating multimodal large language models on contextual aesthetic assessment, distinguishing between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment. The study reveals that general-purpose MLLMs outperform aesthetic-specialized models on contextual suitability tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing IAA benchmarks only assess intrinsic visual quality or fixed domain criteria, failing to evaluate whether an image is appropriate for specific purposes, audiences, cultural settings, or domain conventions. This gap motivates the need for contextual aesthetic assessment.

Method: AesCanvas comprises two components: (1) CritiqueCanvas with 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images supporting multi-dimensional critique across photography, painting, and virtual imagery; (2) ContextCanvas with 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios evaluating contextual aesthetic suitability. The evaluation protocol tests closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs under unified conditions.

Result: Key findings: (1) Clear separation exists between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment; (2) Reference-based lexical and semantic metrics only partially capture critique quality; (3) Aesthetic specialists remain competitive on selected critique metrics but substantially lag strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas; (4) Aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability; (5) Model decisions may fail to track or ground themselves in decisive contextual visual cues.

Conclusion: Culturally situated, evidence-grounded suitability represents a distinct objective for aesthetic modeling that cannot be captured by intrinsic quality assessment alone. General-purpose MLLMs outperform specialized aesthetic models on contextual judgment tasks.

Abstract: Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have extended Image Aesthetic Assessment (IAA) beyond scalar scores toward interpretable critique and guidance. Yet existing benchmarks mainly assess intrinsic visual quality or fixed domain criteria, leaving open whether an appealing image is appropriate for a specific purpose, audience, cultural setting, or domain convention. We introduce AesCanvas, a unified suite with two complementary components: CritiqueCanvas with 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images supports long-form, multi-dimensional critique across photography, painting, and virtual imagery, whereas ContextCanvas with 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios evaluates contextual aesthetic suitability in realistic use scenarios. Under a unified protocol, we evaluate closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs. Results reveal a clear separation between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment: reference-based lexical and semantic metrics only partially capture critique quality, while aesthetic specialists remain competitive on selected critique metrics yet substantially lag strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas. Further analyses show that aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability and that model decisions may fail to track or ground themselves in decisive contextual visual cues. These findings establish culturally situated, evidence-grounded suitability as a distinct objective for aesthetic modeling.

</details>


### [32] [LiveVVT: High-Fidelity Video Virtual Try-On in Real Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26714)
*Yushe Cao,Shikun Feng,Ruxiang Duan,Liyong Wang,Dianxi Shi,Chun Yu,Junliang Xing*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LiveVVT is a rolling streaming diffusion framework for video virtual try-on that preserves bounded bidirectional modeling within causal recurrent generation, enabling high-fidelity real-time streaming with 26× lower latency and 11× higher throughput compared to existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion-based Video Virtual Try-On (VVT) achieves high visual fidelity through bidirectional spatio-temporal modeling, but complete-clip dependence incurs prohibitive latency and computational overhead in practical continuous deployment. Naively enforcing causality disrupts pretrained bidirectional priors and substantially degrades synthesis quality.

Method: LiveVVT uses a rolling streaming diffusion framework with a fixed-size window that jointly denoises multiple video chunks under bounded look-ahead, preserving local bidirectional interactions while emitting one clean chunk per iteration. Two complementary memories sustain long-term consistency: a bounded temporal memory propagates recent dynamics and occlusion context, while a persistent global appearance memory (constructed from target garment and frontal try-on keyframe) anchors garment details. A progressive distillation framework integrates bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression for causal few-step adaptation, and Collaborative Matching Distillation.

Result: Experiments on paired and unpaired long-sequence benchmarks demonstrate superior generation quality over similarly sized models, with 26× lower latency and 11× higher throughput, enabling high-fidelity real-time streaming VVT.

Conclusion: LiveVVT successfully enables high-fidelity real-time streaming video virtual try-on by combining bounded bidirectional modeling within causal recurrent generation, achieving significant improvements in latency and throughput while maintaining generation quality superior to similarly sized models.

Abstract: Diffusion-based Video Virtual Try-On (VVT) achieves high visual fidelity through bidirectional spatio-temporal modeling, but complete-clip dependence incurs prohibitive latency and computational overhead in practical continuous deployment. Naively enforcing causality disrupts pretrained bidirectional priors and substantially degrades synthesis quality. We introduce LiveVVT, a rolling streaming diffusion framework that preserves bounded bidirectional modeling within causal recurrent generation. Within a fixed-size window, LiveVVT jointly denoises multiple video chunks under bounded look-ahead, preserving local bidirectional interactions while emitting one clean chunk per iteration. Beyond the window, two complementary memories sustain long-term consistency: a bounded temporal memory propagates recent dynamics and occlusion context, whereas a persistent global appearance memory, constructed once from the target garment and a frontal try-on keyframe, anchors garment details and dressed appearance throughout the stream. We further introduce a progressive distillation framework integrating bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression for causal few-step adaptation, and Collaborative Matching Distillation, which couples teacher-distribution matching with rolling flow matching on real videos to align optimization with recurrent inference. Experiments on paired and unpaired long-sequence benchmarks demonstrate superior generation quality over similarly sized models, with $26\times$ lower latency and $11\times$ higher throughput, enabling high-fidelity real-time streaming VVT.

</details>


### [33] [Beyond Atomic Layouts: Compositional Design Understanding with Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26716)
*Yiyang Huang,Zhaowen Wang,Simon Jenni,Jing Shi,Yitian Zhang,Yizhou Wang,Yun Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Layout understanding, or the interpretation of element organization, is essential for document analysis, user interface (UI) creation, and graphic design. While recent vision-language models (VLMs) excel at interpreting atomic layouts composed of independent elements, they struggle with compositional layouts that require reasoning over visually entangled elements within hierarchical multi-layer structures. In this paper, we introduce a new task, compositional layout understanding, and present CoDeLayout, a VQA dataset of ~20K real-world multi-layer layouts annotated with compositional element pairs and design intent. Through empirical analysis on CoDeLayout, we identify two key challenges for existing VLMs: semantic drift between textual metadata and visual content, and structural ambiguity in hierarchical inter-element relationships. To address these challenges, we propose MASON, a post-training paradigm that integrates multimodal alignment (MA) and structural perception (SP). MA enhances element interpretation by grounding metadata-defined elements to their visual counterparts, mitigating semantic drift, while SP models layer-aware inter-element spatial relationships to improve hierarchical understanding and reduce structural ambiguity. Experiments reveal substantial gaps in existing VLMs: even the strongest baseline, GPT-o3, achieves only 79.68% accuracy, whereas Qwen2.5-VL 7B with MASON reaches 91.66%. Notably, MASON surpasses full-data Direct Finetune using only 30% of the training data and scales better with additional data.

</details>


### [34] [Parameter Efficient Continual Learning for Sparse Event-Based Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26720)
*Vaishnavi Nagabhushana,Kartikay Agrawal,Ayon Borthakur*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: sLoTh is a parameter-efficient continual learning framework for pretrained sparse event-based (spiking) vision transformers that freezes the backbone and uses scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and shared neuronal threshold modulation, enabling adaptation without replay buffers by updating less than 1% of model parameters while achieving competitive rehearsal-free performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robotic and edge intelligence systems operate in dynamic environments requiring continuous data processing and model adaptation while preserving previously learned knowledge under strict memory and energy constraints. While parameter-efficient fine-tuning shows promise for continual learning with vision transformers, conventional architectures rely on dense computation and remain costly for real-world deployment. Sparse event-based vision transformers provide energy-efficient event-driven computation, but their continual learning capabilities remain unexplored.

Method: sLoTh freezes the backbone and restricts plasticity to two components: (1) scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and (2) shared neuronal threshold modulation. This approach enables adaptation without replay buffers by updating less than 1% of model parameters.

Result: Experiments across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-100, and ImageNet-R with up to 100 tasks demonstrate competitive rehearsal-free performance in class-incremental learning and online continual learning, while enabling approximately 6.5x lower energy consumption than conventional dense vision transformers.

Conclusion: sLoTh successfully enables parameter-efficient continual learning for sparse event-based vision transformers, achieving a good balance between adaptability, memory efficiency, and energy consumption, making it suitable for deployment in resource-constrained robotic and edge intelligence systems.

Abstract: Robotic and edge intelligence systems operate in dynamic environments where data arrives continuously, requiring models to adapt while preserving previously learned knowledge under strict memory and energy constraints. While parameter-efficient fine-tuning has shown promise for continual learning with vision transformers, conventional architectures rely on dense computation and remain costly for real-world deployment. Sparse event-based vision transformers provide energy-efficient event-driven computation, yet their continual learning capabilities remain largely unexplored. We here introduce sLoTh, a parameter-efficient continual learning framework for pretrained sparse event-based (spiking) vision transformers. sLoTh freezes the backbone and restricts plasticity to scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and shared neuronal threshold modulation, enabling adaptation without replay buffers by updating less than 1% of model parameters. Experiments across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-100, and ImageNet-R with up to 100 tasks demonstrate competitive rehearsal-free performance in class-incremental learning and online continual learning, while enabling approximately 6.5x lower energy consumption than conventional dense vision transformers.

</details>


### [35] [UniGeo: A Multi-modal Large Language Model for Text-Guided Cross-View Geo-Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26722)
*Jiahao Wen,Hang Yu,Zhedong Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniGeo is a unified multimodal large language model for text-guided drone geo-localization that jointly supports geo-semantic understanding, cross-view semantic generation, and candidate-level verification to improve fine-grained localization from natural language queries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-guided drone geo-localization faces challenges due to incomplete queries and highly similar candidates, making global cross-modal matching insufficient for reliable fine-grained localization.

Method: UniGeo uses a shared vision-language framework with three key components: (1) geo-semantic learning to establish correspondences among local scene elements, spatial relations, and language descriptions; (2) cross-view generation to model semantic mappings between drone and satellite views; (3) a plug-and-play verification module for fine-grained discrimination among confusable candidates. Training uses a multi-stage strategy progressing through geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification.

Result: On GeoText-1652, UniGeo improves R@10 by 13.59 percentage points and mAP by 2.83 percentage points, demonstrating consistent improvements across multiple retrieval backbones.

Conclusion: UniGeo effectively addresses fine-grained text-guided drone geo-localization through unified multimodal learning combining geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification, achieving significant performance gains over existing methods.

Abstract: Text-guided drone geo-localization aims to identify a target region in a large-scale image gallery from a natural-language description. Existing methods mainly formulate this task as direct matching between an open-ended text query and candidate images. However, incomplete queries and highly similar candidates often make global cross-modal matching insufficient for reliable fine-grained localization. We propose UniGeo, a unified multimodal large language model (MLLM) for text-guided drone geo-localization. Built on a shared vision-language framework, UniGeo jointly supports geo-semantic understanding, cross-view semantic generation, and candidate-level verification. Specifically, it establishes stable correspondences among local scene elements, spatial relations, and language descriptions through geo-semantic learning, and further models semantic mappings between drone and satellite views through cross-view generation. Based on these capabilities, a plug-and-play verification module performs fine-grained discrimination among highly confusable candidates. We further introduce a multi-stage training strategy that progressively learns geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification, improving adaptation to text-guided geo-localization. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple retrieval backbones. On GeoText-1652, UniGeo improves R@10 and mAP by 13.59 and 2.83 percentage points, respectively, validating its effectiveness for fine-grained text-guided drone geo-localization.

</details>


### [36] [GeoMAD: Geometry-Aware Multi-View Anomaly Detection via Deformable Fusion and Distributional Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26724)
*Shang-Fu Chen,Jhih-Ciang Wu,Kuan-Chuan Peng,Wen-Huang Cheng,Kai-Lung Hua*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoMAD is a unified multi-view, multi-class anomaly detection framework that addresses geometric correspondence deficiency and distributional inconsistency in industrial defect detection. It uses a Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module (CDFM) for hierarchical cross-view correspondence without 3D supervision, and a Distributional View Alignment (DVA) loss for global consistency, achieving strong performance on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-view anomaly detection faces a trade-off between geometric awareness and scalability. Existing voxel-based fusion methods require costly 3D construction and class-specific assumptions, while lightweight patch-based methods lack continuous cross-view correspondence. The paper aims to bridge local geometric correspondence and global distributional consistency while preserving 2D feature-space efficiency.

Method: The proposed GeoMAD framework consists of two key components: (1) Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module (CDFM) that learns content-adaptive, view-pair-specific sampling offsets directly on 2D feature maps, arranged across a multi-scale window pyramid with image-global reference sampling to enable hierarchical cross-view correspondence without camera calibration, voxel construction, or class-specific 3D supervision; (2) Distributional View Alignment (DVA), a self-supervised cross-view regularization loss that aligns each view's bottleneck distribution against a per-instance view-centric target, enforcing global consistency without pixel-level correspondence.

Result: GeoMAD achieves strong detection and localization performance on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny benchmarks in unified multi-view multi-class anomaly detection settings.

Conclusion: GeoMAD successfully bridges local geometric correspondence and global distributional consistency in multi-view anomaly detection, providing geometry-aware and distribution-consistent fusion while preserving the efficiency of 2D feature-space learning. The method eliminates the need for camera calibration, voxel construction, or class-specific 3D supervision, making it practical for real-world industrial applications.

Abstract: Multi-view anomaly detection (MvAD) detects defects by exploiting complementary observations from multiple camera viewpoints. The central challenge is to fuse views with sufficient geometric awareness while remaining scalable to multi-class industrial settings. Existing methods typically fall into two extremes: voxel-based fusion provides explicit geometric alignment but requires costly 3D construction and class-specific assumptions, whereas lightweight patch-based fusion is efficient but relies on discrete candidate matching and lacks continuous cross-view correspondence. In this paper, we propose GeoMAD, a unified multi-view, multi-class AD framework that addresses both geometric correspondence deficiency and distributional inconsistency. Our \textit{Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module} (CDFM) learns content-adaptive, view-pair-specific sampling offsets directly on 2D feature maps and arranges them across a multi-scale window pyramid with image-global reference sampling, enabling hierarchical cross-view correspondence without camera calibration, voxel construction, or class-specific 3D supervision. We further introduce \textit{Distributional View Alignment} (DVA), a self-supervised cross-view regularization loss that aligns each view's bottleneck distribution against a per-instance view-centric target, enforcing global consistency without pixel-level correspondence. Together, CDFM and DVA bridge local geometric correspondence and global distributional consistency, providing geometry-aware and distribution-consistent fusion while preserving the efficiency of 2D feature-space learning. Extensive experiments on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny show that GeoMAD achieves strong detection and localization performance in unified MvAD.

</details>


### [37] [Generative Semantic Scene Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26737)
*Shi Chen,Weifeng Ge*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Outdoor LiDAR semantic scene completion (SSC) recovers a dense semantic voxel grid from a scan observing 1% of the target volume, under class imbalance beyond 7,000x. We recast SSC as generative semantic scene completion (GSSC): a single discrete-diffusion formulation in three roles. First, paired sparse-dense scene synthesis (PS$^3$) generates matched sparse LiDAR observations with their dense semantic completions, addressing the long tail at its source and yielding the PS$^3$-SemanticKITTI corpus we train on alongside SemanticKITTI. Second, semantic-guided generative scene completion (SGSC) generates the scene from noise with multinomial discrete diffusion, conditioned on the sparse scan through a bird's-eye-view semantic map and a sparse 3D feature stream. Third, the same framework instead refines an existing completion in one flow-matching step: structured source discrete diffusion (S$^2$D$^2$). S$^2$D$^2$ improves the mIoU of SGSC's own output and every external SSC base tested, without base retraining or test-time adaptation. On the strongest base, one step without test-time augmentation reaches 38.8% mIoU on the SemanticKITTI hidden test. To our knowledge that is the best causal, single-sweep, single-sample result on that leaderboard, +2.1 pp over the previous best published score under the same restriction. Four correction steps with eight-view test-time augmentation reach 39.2%, outside that restriction.

</details>


### [38] [G2D: Generative-to-Discriminative Collaborative Inference for Zero-Shot Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26744)
*Zehua Hao,Fang Liu,Qinliang Wang,Yaoyang Du,Xinyan Huang,Puhua Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: G2D is a training-free zero-shot classification framework that combines CLIP's discriminative retrieval with a generative VLM's fine-grained verification. It addresses CLIP's failure mode (correct label often in top-K but not top-1) and generative models' limitations (large label spaces, unconstrained outputs) by using CLIP for candidate retrieval and a VLM to verify those candidates against the image.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Zero-shot classification requires both efficient label retrieval and fine-grained visual reasoning. Discriminative models like CLIP fail when the correct label isn't in the top-1 prediction, though it often remains in the top-K shortlist. Generative vision-language models struggle with large label spaces and unconstrained outputs. This complementarity suggests combining both approaches: CLIP for broad candidate retrieval, and generative VLM for fine-grained verification.

Method: G2D uses CLIP to retrieve top-K candidates, then employs a generative VLM to verify these candidates against the image. Key techniques include: (1) Fixed confidence routing to focus generative reasoning on uncertain samples, (2) Entropy-adaptive candidate sizing to adjust the number of candidates based on prediction confidence, and (3) Trie-constrained decoding to ensure valid outputs. Candidate names and CLIP probabilities provide a structured prior for resolving visually similar classes.

Result: G2D achieves 68.85% average accuracy across eight benchmarks, compared to 59.35% for CLIP and 63.11% for the standalone VLM. Across seven generator configurations, candidate-set verification improves average accuracy by 1.08-27.42 percentage points. G2D also transfers to other CLIP variants including DCLIP, WaffleCLIP, and CuPL.

Conclusion: The proposed G2D framework successfully bridges discriminative proposal and generative visual reasoning for zero-shot classification. By combining CLIP's efficient retrieval with a generative VLM's verification capabilities, it achieves significant improvements over both approaches individually. The training-free nature and transferability to other CLIP variants suggest practical applicability.

Abstract: Zero-shot classification needs efficient label retrieval and fine-grained visual reasoning, yet discriminative and generative vision-language models fail in complementary ways.When CLIP's top-1 prediction is wrong, the correct label often remains in its top-$K$ shortlist, making disambiguation rather than recall the key challenge.Standalone generative models, however, are hindered by large label spaces and unconstrained outputs.This complementarity motivates separating broad candidate retrieval from fine-grained, image-grounded verification.We propose G2D, a training-free framework that uses a generative VLM to verify CLIP-retrieved candidates against the image.Candidate names and CLIP probabilities provide a structured prior for resolving visually similar classes.Fixed confidence routing, entropy-adaptive candidate sizing, and trie-constrained decoding focus generative reasoning on uncertain samples and ensure one valid output for each input at test time.Across eight benchmarks, G2D achieves 68.85% average accuracy, versus 59.35% for CLIP and 63.11% for the standalone VLM.Across seven generator configurations, candidate-set verification improves average accuracy by 1.08--27.42 percentage points.G2D also transfers to DCLIP, WaffleCLIP, and CuPL, supporting a practical interface between discriminative proposal and generative visual reasoning. Code: https://github.com/Harzva/G2D

</details>


### [39] [Glass Surface Detection Grounded in 3D Visual Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26752)
*Yiwei Lu,Ke Xu,Tao Yan,Xiaojun Chang,Radu Timofte,Rynson W. H. Lau*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 3D geometry-grounded approach for glass surface detection (GSD), shifting from traditional 2D appearance-based methods to explicit 3D modeling. The method uses VGGT-derived 3D priors and a novel detection head with two modules: FSAM for spectral feature identification and GeGB for 3D geometry grounding. Achieves SOTA on 7 benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing GSD methods rely on 2D appearance cues which fail in geometrically ambiguous scenes due to glass transparency and reflectivity. There was a need for a paradigm shift to 3D visual geometry to explicitly model the physical existence of glass surfaces.

Method: 1) Distill rich 3D priors from VGGT (visual geometry grounded transformer); 2) Generate glass-aware 3D representations; 3) Multi-tasking learning with novel glass detection head containing: Frequency Self-Attention Module (FSAM) - identifies glass-specific spectral features for localization; Geometry Grounding Block (GeGB) - selectively grounds 2D features in 3D geometry for segmentation.

Result: State-of-the-art performance across seven standard GSD benchmarks; generalizes well to video/multi-modal data; substantially improves reconstruction in glass scenes.

Conclusion: The 3D geometry-grounded approach effectively addresses glass surface detection by leveraging 3D priors and specialized modules. The method demonstrates superior performance and generalization capabilities, with code publicly available.

Abstract: Glass surface detection (GSD) is critical for scene understanding and reconstruction, and yet remains challenging due to the transparency and reflectivity of glass surfaces. Existing GSD methods typically rely on 2D appearance cues, which may fail in geometrically ambiguous scenes. In this paper, we propose a paradigm shift: grounding GSD in 3D visual geometry to explicitly model the physical existence of glass surfaces. Our method first distills rich 3D priors from the visual geometry grounded transformer (VGGT) and generates glass-aware 3D representations. It then exploits multi-tasking learning with a novel glass detection head, consisting of two core modules: a Frequency Self-Attention Module (FSAM) that identifies glass-specific spectral features for glass surface localization, and a Geometry Grounding Block (GeGB) that selectively grounds 2D features in 3D geometry for glass surface segmentation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance across seven standard GSD benchmarks, generalizes well to video/multi-modal data, and substantially improves reconstruction in glass scenes. Code is available in https://github.com/YT3DVision/VGGT_GLASS.

</details>


### [40] [Cross-Architecture Knowledge Distillation from a Vision Foundation Model to a Lightweight Visual State Space Model for Tea Leaf Disease Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26771)
*Zibo Zhou,Zongsen Qiu,Rui Chen,Yujie Yao,Yue Zhou,Jianjun Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes cross-architecture knowledge distillation from a DINOv2 Vision Transformer teacher to a compact bidirectional Visual State Space Model (LVSSM) student for tea leaf disease classification on edge devices. The 4.45M-parameter student achieves 95.41% accuracy (98.3% of teacher's accuracy) with 5x fewer parameters, addressing training stability issues through architectural modifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deploying accurate models on edge devices for precision agriculture is challenging due to tight compute budgets. Self-supervised vision foundation models like DINOv2 are too large, while lightweight models trained from scratch on small agricultural datasets underfit.

Method: Cross-architecture knowledge distillation from fine-tuned DINOv2 teacher (Vision Transformer) to compact LVSSM student. Identified and fixed two training stability problems: (1) replaced single large patch-embedding convolution with progressive convolutional stem, (2) fixed severed residual path in fusion layer using gated bidirectional selective-scan block. Used temperature-scaled logit distillation.

Result: Temperature-scaled logit distillation raised test accuracy from 92.32±2.14% to 95.41±1.17% (best single run: 96.20%; macro-F1: 94.45%), a +3.09 percentage-point mean gain. Student uses 5.0 times fewer parameters than the 22M-parameter teacher while retaining 98.3% of its accuracy. Intermediate feature-alignment losses reduced accuracy compared to simple logit-level KD.

Conclusion: Cross-architecture KD from Vision Transformer to SSM-based student is viable for edge deployment. Simple logit-level KD outperforms feature-alignment losses. The 4.45M-parameter LVSSM student achieves competitive accuracy with significantly reduced computational cost.

Abstract: Automated tea leaf disease classification supports precision agriculture, yet deploying accurate models on edge devices remains challenging under tight compute budgets. Self-supervised vision foundation models such as DINOv2 provide strong features but are too large for field deployment, while lightweight models trained from scratch on small agricultural datasets often underfit. We study cross-architecture knowledge distillation (KD) from a fine-tuned DINOv2 teacher (Vision Transformer) to a compact bidirectional Visual State Space Model (LVSSM) student, an underexplored direction because the architectures use fundamentally different token-mixing mechanisms. We identify and fix two training-stability problems that prevent the from-scratch SSM student from learning on limited data: a single large patch-embedding convolution and a fusion layer that severs the residual path. With a progressive convolutional stem and gated bidirectional selective-scan block, the 4.45M-parameter student trains stably. Across three seeds, temperature-scaled logit distillation raises test accuracy from 92.32+/-2.14% to 95.41+/-1.17% (best single run: 96.20%; macro-F1: 94.45%), a +3.09 percentage-point mean gain. The student uses 5.0 times fewer parameters than the 22M-parameter teacher while retaining 98.3% of its accuracy. Ablations show that intermediate feature-alignment losses reduce accuracy, making simple logit-level KD the strongest configuration. A fair from-scratch comparison shows the gain is specific to students that start below the teacher. We report per-class metrics, confusion matrices, bootstrap confidence intervals, and FLOPs/latency measurements, and discuss limitations including the single-dataset scope and simplified non-official SSM implementation.

</details>


### [41] [Ring Forcing: Towards Precise Long-Term Memory for Autoregressive Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26794)
*Bowen Xue,Brandon Y. Feng,Chenguo Lin,Yuchen Lin,Yujia Zeng,Lvmin Zhang,Maneesh Agrawala,Honglei Yan,Panwang Pan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses a critical bottleneck in long-duration video generation: the lack of robust long-term memory in current models. The authors identify two key challenges—object permanence (accurately reproducing object appearances) and memory capacity (processing ultra-long context). They propose Ring Forcing, an autoregressive video diffusion framework featuring ring-structured training, compression/timestep composition for extended memory span, and sparse RoPE for flexible memory adaptation. Experiments show superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence compared to state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scaling video generation to long durations reveals that current models lack robust long-term memory. This manifests in two critical aspects: (1) Object permanence - the inability to precisely reproduce object appearances upon re-entry, and (2) Memory capacity - the inability to process ultra-long context and use information from distant history. Without both capabilities, models cannot maintain temporal coherence over extended video sequences.

Method: The paper proposes Ring Forcing, an autoregressive video diffusion framework with three key innovations: (1) Ring-structured training strategy that enforces retrieval from distant history, reconciling the trade-off between strict historical adherence and generative diversity; (2) Compression and timestep composition strategy that extends effective historical span to minutes-long durations under fixed sequence length constraints; (3) Sparse RoPE mechanism that enables flexible, scalable memory adaptation while fully exploiting pre-trained priors.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that Ring Forcing achieves superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods in long-duration video generation.

Conclusion: The work shows that robust long-term memory requires both object permanence and sufficient context handling. Ring Forcing successfully addresses this challenge through its ring-structured training, compression strategy, and sparse RoPE mechanism, enabling minutes-long video generation with maintained identity and temporal coherence.

Abstract: Scaling video generation to long durations reveals a critical bottleneck: current models lack robust long-term memory. This deficiency can be studied along two critical aspects: object permanence, the ability to precisely reproduce the appearance of objects upon re-entry; and memory capacity, the ability to process ultra-long context and use information from distant history. Robust long-term memory requires both: object permanence without sufficient context handling limits the temporal scope, while long context length without permanence fails to maintain identity. To address this, we present Ring Forcing, an autoregressive video diffusion framework designed to robustly construct and precisely utilize long-term memory. Our ring-structured training strategy enforces retrieval from distant history, effectively reconciling the trade-off between strict historical adherence and generative diversity. To expand memory capacity, we introduce a compression and timestep composition strategy. Under fixed sequence length constraints, this method extends the effective historical span to minutes-long durations and achieves a comprehensive receptive field over the entire history. Furthermore, we present a sparse RoPE mechanism to enable flexible, scalable memory adaptation while fully exploiting pre-trained priors. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Ring Forcing achieves superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods.

</details>


### [42] [Multi-Image Visual Token Pruning in Large Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26806)
*Rongyang Zhang,Chengqiang Lu,Cong Li,Hongchao Gu,Tingjia Shen,Xuyang Zhi,Qimeng Wang,Yan Gao,Yi Wu,Yao Hu,Hao Wang,Enhong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free Adaptive Visual Token Pruning (AVTP) framework that dynamically adjusts pruning layers and ratios across different LVLM architectures to address computational and context length constraints in multi-image scenarios, achieving 2x speedup while maintaining 96.1% accuracy on Qwen3VL-8B.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual token pruning methods rely on static strategies that struggle to adapt across different LVLM architectures and multi-image scenarios, and are constrained by their dependence on attention computations incompatible with efficient techniques like FlashAttention.

Method: AVTP uses training-free adaptive pruning: (1) Strategically determines pruning layers based on empirical analysis of visual attention distributions across various LVLMs, (2) Implements adaptive pruning ratios in multi-image contexts where higher importance images retain proportionally more tokens.

Result: Qwen3VL-8B: 2x inference speedup while maintaining 96.1% original accuracy on multiple multi-image benchmarks; InternVL3.5-8B: retains 94.1% accuracy; LLaVA-OV-7B: exceeds original baseline performance.

Conclusion: AVTP demonstrates effectiveness and robustness across different LVLM architectures, achieving significant inference speedup while maintaining high accuracy, and even improving performance on some models.

Abstract: With the growing demand for processing multiple image sequences in real-world applications, various visual token pruning methods have emerged to mitigate the computational and context length constraints faced by Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs). However, most existing pruning approaches rely on static strategies that struggle to adapt across different architectural LVLMs and multi-image scenarios, and are additionally constrained by their dependence on attention computations that are incompatible with efficient techniques like FlashAttention. To address these limitations, we propose a training-free, Adaptive Visual Token Pruning (AVTP) framework, applicable to diverse LVLM architectures. We strategically determine pruning layers based on empirical analysis of visual attention distributions across various LVLMs, and implement adaptive pruning ratios in multi-image contexts where images of higher importance retain proportionally more tokens. We conduct extensive experiments across different LVLMs to demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of AVTP. Specifically, Qwen3VL-8B achieves 2 times inference speedup while maintaining 96.1\% of its original accuracy on multiple multi-image benchmarks, InternVL3.5-8B retains 94.1\% accuracy, and LLaVA-OV-7B even exceeds its original baseline performance. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/zry13/AVTP}{this link}.

</details>


### [43] [Hyperspectral Diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI): A Self-supervised Framework for Hyperspectral Image Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26812)
*Shuo Li,Mike Davies,Mehrdad Yaghoobi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel Hyperspectral diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI) framework for hyperspectral image (HSI) inpainting that learns directly from a single corrupted acquisition at test time, without requiring large-scale pretraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional diffusion-based methods rely on large-scale pretraining, making them inflexible for different sensor configurations. In practical remote sensing scenarios, large annotated hyperspectral datasets are limited. The problem is ill-posed in unsupervised inpainting settings.

Method: HyDiff-EI embeds equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process by leveraging inherent geometric symmetries and intrinsic characteristics of HSIs, bridging generative diffusion modeling with self-consistent physical priors.

Result: Extensive experiments on real-world datasets (Chikusei, Botswana, and EMIT) demonstrate remarkable inpainting quality over existing self-supervised and diffusion-based algorithms in both noiseless and noisy cases. The approach substantially enhances noise robustness and generalizability.

Conclusion: Coupling diffusion modeling with equivariant priors effectively addresses the ill-posed nature of unsupervised HSI inpainting, providing a flexible and robust solution suitable for practical remote sensing applications with limited data.

Abstract: A novel Hyperspectral diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI) framework for solving the hyperspectral image (HSI) inpainting problem has been presented here. Unlike conventional diffusion-based methods that rely on large-scale pretraining, HyDiff-EI is a test-time optimization framework that learns directly from a single corrupted HSI acquisition. This makes it flexible for different sensor configurations and particularly well-suited for practical remote sensing scenarios where large annotated hyperspectral datasets are limited. To address the ill-posed nature of unsupervised inpainting, we embed equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process. By leveraging the inherent geometric symmetries and intrinsic characteristics of HSIs, HyDiff-EI bridges the gap between generative diffusion modeling and self-consistent physical priors. We empirically show that coupling diffusion modeling with equivariant priors substantially enhances noise robustness and generalizability. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets including Chikusei, Botswana, and EMIT demonstrate that HyDiff-EI offers remarkable inpainting quality over existing self-supervised and diffusion-based algorithms in both noiseless and noisy cases.

</details>


### [44] [LLaVAFlow: Preserving Latent Alignment Flow for Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26820)
*Muyao Yuan,Muyan Jiao,Jiangyong Ying,Weizhan Zhang,Yuanhong Zhang,Lan Ma,Yuan Gao,Haipeng Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: While Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) exhibit strong generalization, visual instruction tuning for downstream tasks inevitably causes catastrophic forgetting, impairing overall generalization. While existing methods regulate weight updates to reduce forgetting, they overlook the fundamental cross-modal alignment in MLLMs. Based on prior work and our observations, we argue that cross-modal alignment is implicitly captured in the information-compression trajectory. To preserve the alignment flow embedded in the trajectory, we propose LLaVAFlow, an information-theoretic distillation framework. First, we compress the mutual information between the extracted relations and MLLM embeddings, encouraging a learnable module to produce a refined alignment flow that benefits downstream tasks. Second, we maximize the mutual information between the extracted alignment flows of the pretrained and fine-tuned MLLMs, enabling the transfer of compact alignment information. Extensive experiments show that LLaVAFlow is an effective plug-and-play framework that preserves alignment flow and enhances both downstream performance and generalization.

</details>


### [45] [Evaluator-Dependent Patient-Adaptive ECG Lead-Channel Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26827)
*Xiaoyang Li,Zeyan Tao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper investigates evaluator dependence in adaptive ECG channel selection policies. When policies trained with a controlled logistic evaluator are evaluated with a stronger ResNet1D evaluator, the performance advantage of adaptive policies reverses—all 30 interaction estimates show positive values, indicating that fixed protocols outperform adaptive policies under the stronger evaluator. This suggests that ECG acquisition policies must be jointly optimized with their diagnostic backbone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Patient-conditioned acquisition policies for ECG lead selection can outperform fixed protocols, but the value of each channel depends on the downstream diagnostic evaluator. The authors investigate whether policies trained under one evaluator transfer when the evaluator is replaced.

Method: The authors froze two policies (ECG-on-Demand and MGA) trained with a controlled arbitrary-mask logistic evaluator, then scored their acquisition trajectories with a more predictive masked raw-waveform ResNet1D. They used exhaustive search to provide metric-matched population-wide fixed comparators for each evaluator, enabling a clean interaction contrast. Three sensitivity analyses were conducted: common-reference scoring, training the strong evaluator on a mixture of policy-generated and random masks, and evaluator-aligned Strong-MGA policy training.

Result: At budget k=4, ECG-on-Demand shifted from D_C = -0.011 (favoring adaptive under the controlled evaluator) to D_S = +0.029 (favoring fixed under the strong evaluator), yielding an NLL interaction of +0.041 (95% CI [+0.030, +0.050]). Across two policies, five budgets, and three probabilistic metrics, all 30 interaction estimates were positive with paired confidence intervals excluding zero. All three sensitivity analyses preserved a positive interaction interval. Evaluator-aligned training reduced but did not eliminate the gap.

Conclusion: Adaptive ECG channel allocation should be developed and validated jointly with its intended diagnostic backbone. Jointly optimized sensing-diagnosis systems remain an open problem. The reference-choice and mask-distribution artifacts are less plausible explanations for the observed interaction.

Abstract: Patient-conditioned acquisition policies for ECG lead-channel selection can outperform population-wide fixed protocols by tailoring the channel budget to each patient's observed cardiac state. However, the value of acquiring any given channel is defined relative to a downstream diagnostic evaluator, so marginal utilities learned under one evaluator need not transfer when the evaluator is replaced. We study this evaluator dependence empirically on PTB-XL by freezing two policies (ECG-on-Demand and MGA) trained with a controlled arbitrary-mask logistic evaluator, then scoring their unchanged acquisition trajectories with a more predictive masked raw-waveform ResNet1D. Exhaustive search provides metric-matched population-wide fixed comparators separately for each evaluator, enabling a clean interaction contrast. At budget $k=4$ on a held-out evaluation fold, ECG-on-Demand shifts from $D_\mathrm{C}=-0.011$ (favoring adaptive under the controlled evaluator) to $D_\mathrm{S}=+0.029$ (favoring fixed under the strong evaluator), yielding an NLL interaction of $+0.041$ (95% CI $[+0.030, +0.050]$). Across two policies, five budgets, and three probabilistic metrics, all 30 interaction estimates are positive with paired confidence intervals excluding zero. Three post-hoc sensitivity analyses -- common-reference scoring, training the strong evaluator on a mixture of policy-generated and random masks, and evaluator-aligned Strong-MGA policy training -- each preserve a positive interaction interval, making reference-choice and mask-distribution artifacts less plausible explanations. Evaluator-aligned training reduces but does not eliminate the gap. These results indicate that adaptive ECG channel allocation should be developed and validated jointly with its intended diagnostic backbone, and that jointly optimized sensing-diagnosis systems remain an open problem.

</details>


### [46] [Rethinking Image Processing for the Age of AI: A Problem-First Framework for Scientific Progress](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26833)
*Guoping Qiu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This position paper critiques the prevailing "model-first" research paradigm in AI-powered image processing, where researchers start with available architectures and optimize on benchmarks rather than addressing real-world imaging problems. The authors advocate for a "problem-first" approach and propose a six-stage workflow, using super-resolution and low-light enhancement as case studies to demonstrate how benchmarks can diverge from actual problems. The paper calls for improved research standards and cultural change.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors observe that the availability of powerful AI models, public datasets, and benchmark leaderboards has encouraged researchers to optimize existing architectures on benchmarks rather than solving actual real-world imaging problems. This trend produces impressive benchmark results but fails to advance genuine understanding or solutions to practical problems.

Method: The paper uses case studies of super-resolution and low-light enhancement to illustrate how benchmark datasets may define tasks substantially different from the real-world problems they intend to represent. It proposes a six-stage workflow that prioritizes problem formulation, image acquisition, information-loss analysis, assumptions, ambiguity, and evaluation before model and dataset selection.

Result: The paper demonstrates that benchmark-defined tasks can differ substantially from real-world imaging problems, and that performance improvements must be interpreted within the specific conditions under which they are obtained. The six-stage workflow provides a structured approach to ensure research addresses genuine problems.

Conclusion: The paper calls for a fundamental change in research culture and education, emphasizing deeper understanding of imaging problems and more appropriate use of modern AI to achieve genuine scientific and technical advancement. It proposes clearer standards for evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty, and claims of state-of-the-art performance.

Abstract: Modern AI has greatly expanded the capabilities of image processing. However, the ready availability of powerful models, public datasets, and benchmark leaderboards has also en- couraged a model-first research pattern: researchers increasingly begin with an available architecture and optimize it on a public benchmark, rather than beginning with the underlying real-world imaging problem. This can produce impressive benchmark results without necessarily improving our understanding or solution of the real problem. This paper argues for a problem-first approach that distinguishes the physical imaging problem, solution principle, statistical estimator, and computational implementation, while clarifying what modern AI can achieve and which fundamental problems remain unsolved. Through case studies of super- resolution and low-light enhancement, we show how benchmark datasets may define tasks that differ substantially from the real-world problems they are intended to represent, and why performance improvements must be interpreted within the conditions under which they are obtained. We propose a six-stage workflow that places problem formulation, image acquisition, information-loss analysis, assumptions, ambiguity, and evaluation before model and dataset selection. The paper also proposes clearer standards for evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty, and claims of state-of-the-art performance. More fundamentally, it calls for a change in research culture and education so that future researchers learn to understand imaging problems deeply and use modern AI to achieve genuine scientific and technical advancement.

</details>


### [47] [MedFG-VQA: Low-Frequency Memory and Graph Attention for Lightweight Medical VQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26848)
*Haowen Gu,Gensheng Pei,Zeren Sun,Mingwu Ren,Xiangbo Shu,Yazhou Yao,Fumin Shen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedFG-VQA is a lightweight medical VQA framework that uses memory-augmented low-frequency features and graph-enhanced cross-attention to achieve efficient visual-textual alignment, addressing data scarcity through a large synthetic dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical VQA faces two key challenges: limited annotated training data and the high computational demands of existing large vision-language models, which hinder clinical deployment.

Method: Two key components: (1) Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF) augments DCT-based low-frequency features by retrieving from a learnable memory bank; (2) Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA) aligns visual-textual features through cross-attention and refines them via graph-convolutional aggregation. Data scarcity is addressed by constructing SynMed-VQA, a synthetic dataset with 2M+ QA pairs across 9 imaging modalities and 10 organs generated using GPT-4o.

Result: MedFG-VQA achieves competitive or superior performance on SynMed-VQA and three other biomedical VQA benchmarks compared to much larger models, while maintaining significantly lower computational costs.

Conclusion: The proposed framework demonstrates that efficient, lightweight models can achieve strong performance in medical VQA, showing potential for clinical deployment due to its efficiency and effectiveness.

Abstract: Medical Visual Question Answering (Med-VQA) holds significant promise for clinical decision support, yet faces challenges due to limited annotated data and the high computational demands of existing large vision-language models. We propose MedFG-VQA, a lightweight framework that leverages a memory bank to augment DCT-based low-frequency features and employs graph-enhanced cross-attention for effective visual-textual alignment. Specifically, our approach features two key components: Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF), which enhances low-frequency features by retrieving from a learnable memory bank built on DCT decomposition, and Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA), which aligns visual-textual features via cross-attention and refines them through graph-convolutional aggregation. To address data scarcity, we construct SynMed-VQA, a large-scale synthetic dataset comprising over 2 million question-answer pairs across 9 imaging modalities and 10 major organs, generated with GPT-4o. Extensive experiments on SynMed-VQA and three other standard biomedical VQA benchmarks demonstrate that MedFG-VQA achieves competitive or superior performance compared to much larger models while maintaining significantly lower computational costs, highlighting its efficiency and potential for clinical deployment.

</details>


### [48] [From Reasoning to Pixels: Grounded Medical Multimodal LLMs for VQA and Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26856)
*Haowen Gu,Gensheng Pei,Junzhu Mao,Qiong Wang,Mingwu Ren,Yazhou Yao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedREAL addresses the limitation of Multimodal LLMs in medical VQA by lacking pixel-level grounding. It introduces SARP for extracting semantic evidence from [SEG] tokens and R2V fusion for segmentation. The method achieves 68.49% gIoU and 70.47% cIoU, trained on a new dataset MedRAVS-13K with 13,824 samples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current MLLMs in Med-VQA rely on global image features and lack precise pixel-level grounding, limiting clinical trustworthiness. There is a semantic gap between high-level clinical reasoning and spatial localization.

Method: 1) SegAnchored Reasoning Pooling (SARP) to distill task-relevant semantic evidence from [SEG] tokens within MLLM's hidden states. 2) Reasoning-to-Visual (R2V) fusion mechanism to inject reasoning-aware features into segmentation pipeline for accurate mask decoding. 3) Construction of MedRAVS-13K dataset with 13,824 samples across four imaging modalities.

Result: MedREAL significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving 68.49% gIoU and 70.47% cIoU on benchmark evaluations. The framework generates evidence masks strictly consistent with textual diagnoses.

Conclusion: MedREAL provides a robust, interpretable framework for reasoning-driven medical image analysis by seamlessly aligning linguistic reasoning with spatial grounding, addressing the clinical trustworthiness issue in existing MLLMs.

Abstract: Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated impressive performance in Medical Visual Question Answering (Med-VQA), their reliance on global image features often lacks precise pixel-level grounding, thereby limiting clinical trustworthiness. To bridge the semantic gap between high-level clinical reasoning and spatial localization, we propose \textsc{\textsc{MedREAL}} (\textbf{Med}ical \textbf{RE}asoning-driven \textbf{A}nswering and \textbf{L}ocalization), a unified framework that seamlessly aligns linguistic reasoning with spatial grounding. Specifically, \textsc{MedREAL} introduces \textbf{S}eg \textbf{A}nchored \textbf{R}easoning \textbf{P}ooling (SARP) to distill task-relevant semantic evidence directly from \texttt{[SEG]} tokens within the MLLM's hidden states. Furthermore, a \textbf{R}easoning-to-\textbf{V}isual (R2V) fusion mechanism is proposed to effectively inject these reasoning-aware features into a segmentation pipeline for accurate mask decoding. To facilitate this paradigm, we construct MedRAVS-13K, a comprehensive dataset comprising 13,824 expertly validated samples across four diverse imaging modalities. Extensive experiments demonstrate that \textsc{MedREAL} significantly outperforms state-of-the-arts, achieving 68.49\% gIoU and 70.47\% cIoU on benchmark evaluations. By generating evidence masks that are strictly consistent with textual diagnoses, \textsc{MedREAL} provides a robust, interpretable framework for reasoning-driven medical image analysis.

</details>


### [49] [A Geometry-Driven, Framework-Agnostic Optimization for Object Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26859)
*Wei Chen,Tao Zhen,Zhongchen Shi,Jing Zhang,Liang Xie,Erwei Yin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a data-centric optimization for object pose estimation using a physically grounded rotation representation through principal axes alignment. The method aligns object coordinate systems with inherent geometric axes derived from inertial properties, providing stability, symmetry handling, and framework compatibility without architectural changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current object pose estimation research is predominantly model-centric, focusing on architectural innovations. This work addresses the need for a geometry-driven approach that can enhance pose estimation without complex network redesigns, while explicitly handling rotational ambiguities for symmetric objects at the data level.

Method: The proposed method aligns the object's coordinate system with its inherent geometric axes derived from inertial properties (principal axes). This optimization is applied purely at the dataset level, ensuring plug-and-play compatibility with existing networks without architectural modifications. The approach provides: 1) Inherent Stability through energy-minimizing property of principal axes, 2) Symmetry-Aware Canonicalization by explicitly resolving rotational ambiguities for symmetric objects, and 3) Framework Agnosticism for plug-and-play compatibility.

Result: Extensive experiments across diverse category-level and instance-level models demonstrate consistent and significant accuracy improvements while preserving the integrity of the baseline network.

Conclusion: This work establishes a new, geometry-driven direction for enhancing pose estimation that circumvents the need for complex network redesign. The data-centric approach through principal axes alignment provides a robust solution that is less sensitive to noise and occlusions, fundamentally eliminates label confusion for symmetric objects, and maintains compatibility with existing architectures.

Abstract: Current object pose estimation research remains predominantly model-centric, focusing on architectural innovations and post-processing refinements. This paper introduces a data-centric optimization by proposing a novel, physically grounded rotation representation through principal axes alignment. Our method aligns the object's coordinate system with its inherent geometric axes, derived from inertial properties, yielding three key advantages: Inherent Stability-leveraging the energy-minimizing property of principal axes provides a robust representation that is less sensitive to noise and occlusions; Symmetry-Aware Canonicalization-explicitly resolving rotational ambiguities for symmetric objects at the data level, which fundamentally eliminates label confusion during network training; and Framework Agnosticism-the optimization is applied purely at the dataset level, ensuring plug-and-play compatibility with existing networks without any architectural modification. We validate the framework across diverse category-level and instance-level models. Extensive experiments demonstrate consistent and significant accuracy improvements, while preserving the integrity of the baseline network. This work establishes a new, geometry-driven direction for enhancing pose estimation, circumventing the need for complex network redesign.

</details>

*Zhiyang Chen,Changchun Yin,Huiqin Yang,Liming Fang*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Self-supervised learning (SSL) models are vulnerable to backdoor attacks. However, the systemic risks they pose in face representation have received little attention. The entanglement of identity features in self-supervised face learning presents unique challenges for attack stealthiness. To address this gap, we propose FIDA (Feature Instability-Driven Attack), a novel backdoor attack framework. FIDA uses subtle semantic triggers for injection, but its key innovation is a novel objective called Feature Instability Loss. It trains the encoder to increase the sensitivity of triggered features along perturbation directions sampled during attack optimization . By preventing the backdoor from exhibiting the rigid feature patterns typical of previous attacks, FIDA effectively evades the evaluated perturbation-based defenses. Experiments show that FIDA achieves a high attack success rate and generally preserves benign utility across the evaluated settings , posing a significant threat to real-world multimedia applications relying on facial analysis.

</details>

### [51] [Emotion Understanding in Streaming Video with Trajectory-Aware Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26786)
*Qingsong Wang,Qigong Lei,Zitong Wang,Bohan Yu,Zhiang Dong,Jian liu,Weiqiang Wang,Chang Yao,Jingyuan Chen*

Main category: cs.MM

TL;DR: Summary generation failed
